Astrophysics - Chapter 41

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 1

Statement: Every optimal transport on 22-manifold with Ricci curvature ≥ 0 satisfies Brascamp-Lieb inequality.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By log-Sobolev inequality and functional inequalities, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. ∎

Theorem 2

Statement: For the Ising model measure on 49-space, the log-Sobolev constant is at most the arithmetic genus p_a.

Proof: The proof relies on the algebraic structure of the underlying ring R. By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, we decompose the module M into indecomposable summands. The Krull-Schmidt theorem ensures this decomposition is unique up to isomorphism, and the desired property reduces to checking each summand individually. ∎

Theorem 3

Statement: The isoperimetric inequality of a Gaussian field with 73-th moment bounded by the intersection form Q gives sub-exponential tails.

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By modified logarithmic Sobolev inequalities, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. ∎

Theorem 13

By the inductive hypothesis, each piece satisfies the property, and entropy method for concentration inequalities ensures these local results glue to the global statement. ∎

Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. ∎

Theorem 994

Statement: For a Langevin diffusion coupling with marginals μ and ν, the cost is at least the arithmetic genus p_a.

Proof: We proceed by strong induction on the invariant c(X). The base case c(X)=0 is trivial. Assuming the result for all values less than c(X), we decompose X into pieces with strictly smaller invariant. By the inductive hypothesis, each piece satisfies the property, and transport-entropy inequalities à la Marton ensures these local results glue to the global statement. ∎

Theorem 996

Statement: A measure with 75-th Ricci tensor bounded below by the Maslov index μ satisfies Rényi divergence.

Proof: We employ the topological structure of the ambient space. By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, the relevant homotopy group πₙ(X) vanishes for the appropriate n, ensuring that the obstruction to extending the partial solution is trivial. An obstruction-theoretic argument then completes the proof. ∎

Theorem 998

Statement: Every Ising model on 82-manifold with Ricci curvature ≥ 0 satisfies Poincaré inequality.

Proof: We formulate the statement in the language of category theory. By entropy power inequality in high dimensions, the functor F preserves the relevant limits and colimits. The universal property of the adjunction (G ⊣ F) translates the existence statement into a computation that can be verified component-wise, establishing the result. ∎

Theorem 999

Statement: The modified log-Sobolev inequality for the measure holds with 18-dependent constant the Maslov index μ.

Proof: We construct the required object explicitly. By entropy method for concentration inequalities, we define the map iteratively: at each stage n, the compatibility condition is ensured by the universal property of the ambient category. The resulting object satisfies all axioms by construction, and its uniqueness follows from the terminality argument. ∎

Theorem 1001

Statement: The 106-dimensional distribution satisfies the isoperimetric inequality with constant the Kodaira dimension κ.

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By sharp isoperimetric inequalities on manifolds, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. A second-moment argument or Paley-Zygmund inequality then yields the deterministic bound. ∎
